<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the complete double-dimer loop ensemble for the Temperleyan square lattice in the upper half-plane converges to nested CLE<sub>4</sub>. The convergence holds along the full mesh limit and matches every macroscopic loop as an unparametrized curve. This resolves the half-plane Temperleyan form of the double-dimer CLE<sub>4</sub> scaling-limit conjecture.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For the half-plane Temperleyan law specified above, the family $F(\mathcal L_\delta)$ is tight as $\delta\downarrow0$ in the loop-collection topology just defined, and $$F(\mathcal L_\delta)\ \Longrightarrow\ F(\mathcal L), \qquad \delta\downarrow0,$$ where $\mathcal L$ is standard nested $\mathrm{CLE}_4$ in $\mathbb H$. The convergence holds along the full mesh limit and matches all macroscopic loops as curves.
  - Explanation: Identifies the full curve scaling limit of the specified half-plane double-dimer ensemble and retains every macroscopic nested loop.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cylindrical)): Let $z_1,\ldots,z_r$ be distinct interior points of $\mathbb H$, and choose face punctures $z_j^\delta\to z_j$. For the two independent matchings used in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the reduced lamination relative to $(z_j^\delta)_{j=1}^r$ converges in law to the corresponding reduced lamination of nested $\mathrm{CLE}_4$. In particular the multisets of enclosed puncture subsets, with multiplicities and with subsets of size at most one deleted, converge in law.
  - Explanation: Imports convergence of reduced puncture records for each fixed interior puncture configuration; it does not identify curve traversals.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cle-facts)): After conformal transport to the disk, nested $\mathrm{CLE}_4$ consists almost surely of pairwise disjoint simple loops contained in the open disk. There are finitely many loops of diameter greater than any positive number. Every fixed interior point is almost surely off all loop traces and is surrounded by infinitely many successive nested loops.
  - Explanation: Records simplicity, disjointness, interior containment, macroscopic finiteness and successive nesting of the target ensemble.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rectangle-crossings)): Let the odd corner-rooted rectangles $R_\delta$ converge to a fixed nondegenerate rectangle. Let $u_\delta<v_\delta$ be parallel cuts in either lattice direction, whose limiting coordinates are strictly inside the longitudinal sides and whose limiting separation is positive. Under two independent uniform matchings of $R_\delta$, $$\lim_{K\to\infty}\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb P\{N(u_\delta,v_\delta)>K\}=0.$$ The traversals are counted over all loops, with no restriction on their portions outside the slab.
  - Explanation: Establishes tightness of total slab traversals, including repeated crossings by the same loop.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-spectrum)): For $n=2p+1$, put $$u_l=\operatorname{arsinh}\!\left(\cos\frac{\pi l}{n+1}\right), \quad 1\le l\le p, \qquad s=\exp\!\left(\sum_{l=1}^p u_l\right).$$ There are orthonormal one-particle bases on alternate seams in which each transfer is diagonal with entries $$e^{u_1},\ldots,e^{u_p},1,e^{-u_p},\ldots,e^{-u_1}.$$ Every basis vector has site coordinates bounded by $C/\sqrt n$, for an absolute constant $C$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:mode-gap} u_l\ge c\frac{p+1-l}{n}$$ for an absolute $c>0$. If $t=b-a$ and $1\le k\le p$, then $$\label{eq:sector-penalty} \frac{\\|T^{(p+k)}_{b,a}\\|\, \\|T^{(p-k)}_{b,a}\\|}{s^{2t}} \le \exp\!\left(-c\frac{t k^2}{n}\right).$$ Finally, assign to any subset $J$ of the one-particle modes the normalized propagation weight $$w_t(J)=s^{-t}\prod_{j\in J}\sigma_j^t,$$ where the $\sigma_j$ are the displayed singular values. For every $\eta>0$, $$\label{eq:all-state-sum} \sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}}w_t(J) =2\prod_{l=1}^p(1+e^{-t u_l})^2\le C_\eta, \qquad t\ge\eta n.$$ The corresponding sum for two replicas is at most $C_\eta^2$.
  - Explanation: Diagonalizes alternating transfers and supplies sector penalties and summable normalized propagation weights.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cap-comparison)): Fix $\eta,\rho>0$ and integer seams $0\le a\le b\le m$. If $m/n\ge\rho$, $a\ge\eta n$, and $m-b\ge\eta n$, then $$\label{eq:cap-comparison} \frac{\\|L_a\otimes L_a\\|\, \\|R_b\otimes R_b\\|\,s^{2(b-a)}}{Z_{\rm dd}} \le C_{\eta,\rho}.$$ In particular the bound is uniform as the two cuts vary in any fixed longitudinal interior region of a nondegenerate limiting rectangle.
  - Explanation: Controls cap normalization relative to the partition function when seams stay longitudinally interior.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-cap)): Start with an ordinary two-replica cap vector. In each uncolored cap diagram specify a collection of eligible arches, and retain only diagrams having at most $M$ eligible arches. Sum over ordered lists of $k$ distinct eligible arches, with one positive or negative tear prescribed on each marked arch, and give every list any weight in $[0,1]$ depending only on the uncolored diagram and its marks. The resulting vector $V$ satisfies $$\label{eq:marked-cap-norm} \\|V\\|\le M^k\\|V_{\rm ordinary}\\|.$$ Moreover, when such vectors are joined by ordinary double-replica transfer, their expansion over uncolored configurations has precisely the ordinary edge multiplicities. Dividing a contraction by $Z_{\rm dd}$ therefore gives the expectation of the ratio of modified color counts to ordinary color counts, with the prescribed mark weights. The same interpretation holds for an inserted linear operation that preserves the total seam-edge multiplicity at each site; signs of that operation are included in the modified count.
  - Explanation: Bounds marked cap vectors and interprets contractions as modified color counts relative to ordinary counts.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rectangle-short)): Let $R_\delta$ be corner-rooted odd lattice rectangles converging to a fixed nondegenerate rectangle, with two independent uniform dimer covers. Fix an interior longitudinal coordinate $c$, two positive numbers $\rho_-,\rho_+$, and a bounded segment $J$ of the transverse line $x=c$. The two regions $x\le c-\rho_-$ and $x\ge c+\rho_+$ are called the deep regions; if either is disjoint from the rectangle, the events below are empty. The segment $J$ may extend beyond the transverse sides of the rectangle.* *For each sufficiently small $h>0$, partition $J$ into at most $C_J/h$ intervals of length at most $h$, with endpoints off the graph. Use a seam converging to $x=c$ and the corresponding intervals when necessary. A tested arc is a cycle subarc, in its cycle direction, with one endpoint in each deep region. Let $B_{\delta,h}$ be the event that some partition interval $I$ satisfies at least one of the following conditions:* 1. *a tested arc $\alpha$ on a cycle $g$ has $\iota_I(\alpha)\ne0$ and $\iota_I(\alpha)\ne\iota_I(g)$;* 2. *tested arcs on two distinct cycles both have nonzero intersection number with $I$.* *The arcs may otherwise travel anywhere in the rectangle. Then $$\lim_{h\downarrow0}\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb P(B_{\delta,h})=0. \label{eq:rectangle-short-limit}$$ The conclusion is unchanged by truncating intervals at a transverse side, or by placing their endpoints outside the rectangle.
  - Explanation: Excludes incompatible arc indices and simultaneous nonzero indices on distinct cycles through shrinking transverse intervals.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sandwiched-insertion)): Let $\mathcal T_-$ and $\mathcal T_+$ be the two-replica transfers from $a$ to $c$ and from $c$ to $b$, with column lengths $t_-$ and $t_+$. For the fixed positive widths above, so that $t_\pm\ge\eta n$ for some $\eta>0$ and all sufficiently small meshes, $$\bigl\\|s^{-2(t_-+t_+)} \mathcal T_+\mathcal O_I\mathcal T_-\bigr\\| \le C\left(\frac{\|S(I)\|}{n}\right)^2. \label{eq:sandwiched-insertion}$$ Consequently, uniformly over the cap-local weights $\lambda(A)\in[0,1]$, $$\frac{\bigl\|\langle V_R, \mathcal T_+\mathcal O_I\mathcal T_-V_L\rangle\bigr\|} {Z_{\rm dd}} \le C_M\left(\frac{\|S(I)\|}{n}\right)^2. \label{eq:marked-contraction-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Uses delocalized mode projections and bounded propagation sums to control the sandwiched insertion.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-color-contractions)): Fix one eligible mark in each cap, before multiplying their weights.* 1. *If both marks lie on one loop, let $u,v$ be the intersection numbers with $I$ of its two mark-to-mark arcs, both oriented from the left mark to the right mark. Its summed signed color ratio is $uv/2$. This is nonnegative, and is at least $1/2$ when $u,v\ne0$.* 2. *If the marks lie on distinct loops $g,g'$, put $$t_g=\sum_{\text{hits of }g\text{ in }I}\sigma_r.$$ Their summed signed color ratio is $-t_gt_{g'}/4$. The number $t_g$ is zero unless $g$ separates the endpoints of $I$, in which case it is $1$ or $-1$.
  - Explanation: Computes signed color ratios for marks on the same loop and on distinct loops.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cap-weight-decrease)): On $E_M$, if a separating loop $g'$ lies inside another separating loop $g$, then $$U_{g'}\le\tfrac12 U_g, \qquad V_{g'}\le\tfrac12 V_g.$$ In particular a zero outer weight forces all deeper weights in that cap to be zero.
  - Explanation: Shows inward decay of total eligible-arch weights along separating nesting chains.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-chain-positivity)): Suppose a finite family consists of at most two chains, each ordered from outside inward. Give its members signs $t_g\in\{-1,1\}$ which alternate in either chain and have opposite outermost signs when both chains are present. Let $U_g,V_g\ge0$ decrease by a factor of at least two at each step inward. Then the expression <a href="#eq:different-loop-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:different-loop-sum">[eq:different-loop-sum]</a> is nonnegative. If every positive $U_g$ or $V_g$ is at least $q>0$ and there are distinct $g,g'$ with $U_gV_{g'}>0$, the expression is at least $q^2/16$.
  - Explanation: Proves positivity and a conditional lower bound for the complete different-loop contribution.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-localization)): Let $K$ be a bounded closed subset of $\overline{\mathbb H}$ and let $\varepsilon>0$. There is an $R<\infty$ such that, for every sufficiently small $\delta$, the two independent half-plane matchings can be coupled with two independent uniform matchings of $\mathcal R_{\delta,R}$ so that both matching states agree on $K$ with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Couples finite-rectangle and half-plane matching states on a bounded window while preserving independence within each pair.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-walk-attachment)): Fix $1\le C_0<\infty$. There are constants $C_1>C_0$ and $p>0$ with the following property. Let a square-lattice walk have mesh $a_0$, start at $x$ above a horizontal killing boundary, and let $a\ge a_0$. Suppose an already explored nearest-neighbor path starts within distance $C_0a$ of $x$ and runs to that boundary. The probability that the walk hits the path or the killing boundary before leaving $B(x,C_1a)$ is at least $p$. The constants are independent of the path, the mesh, and the distance of $x$ from the boundary.
  - Explanation: Gives a uniform positive attachment probability to an explored branch or killing boundary before a controlled excursion.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-local-crossings)): Fix a bounded window $K\subset\overline{\mathbb H}$ and two distinct parallel coordinate lines. For horizontal lines assume both have positive height. The maximum total number of parameter-disjoint portions of half-plane double-dimer cycles that lie in $K$ and traverse from one line to the other, in either direction, is tight as $\delta\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Transfers traversal tightness to portions lying in a bounded half-plane window.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-local-opposite)): Let $u$ be either the horizontal or vertical coordinate, let $L=\{u=c\}$ be a test line, and let $J\subset L$ be a bounded segment. If $u$ is the vertical coordinate, assume $c>0$. Fix a bounded window $K\subset\overline{\mathbb H}$ and buffers $\rho_-,\rho_+>0$. Deterministically partition $J$ into $O(h^{-1})$ intervals of length at most $h$, with endpoints off the graph. Let $E_{\delta,h}$ be the event that, for one of these intervals $I$, one directed half-plane cycle has two portions $\alpha,\beta$ contained in $K$ such that each portion has one endpoint in $\{u\le c-\rho_-\}$ and the other in $\{u\ge c+\rho_+\}$, and $$\iota_I(\alpha)\iota_I(\beta)<0.$$ Then $$\lim_{h\downarrow0}\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb P(E_{\delta,h})=0. \label{eq:local-opposite-limit}$$ For a vertical line $L$, the segment may reach or extend below the bottom boundary. Test lines are chosen off lattice vertices; the same estimate holds for seam perturbations of order $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Rules out opposite nonzero indices of buffered portions of a directed cycle on short intervals after localization.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-disk-trips)): For every $\eta>0$, the random variables $N_\eta(F(\mathcal L_\delta))$ are tight as $\delta\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Detects macroscopic disk trips in bounded half-plane windows and proves their tightness.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tightness)): The laws of $F(\mathcal L_\delta)$ are tight in the collection space of closed-disk curves with the ensemble matching topology, with constant loops discarded. Moreover, the loops can be ordered by decreasing diameter and given measurable, orientation-preserving parametrizations whose joint laws are tight in the product of uniform path spaces. Every subsequential limit has finitely many loops above every positive diameter, and convergence retains all its nonconstant entries. At this stage limiting curves may be nonsimple, touch the boundary, or occur with multiplicity.
  - Explanation: Obtains tight parametrized representatives and collection compactness while permitting provisional nonsimplicity and boundary contact.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-visible-signatures)): There is a coupling with a standard nested $\mathrm{CLE}_4$ collection $\mathcal C$ such that the limiting entries whose signatures contain at least two points are in bijection with the loops of $\mathcal C$, preserving all memberships in $Q$. Every other nonconstant entry has empty signature.
  - Explanation: Couples visible signatures with canonical nested loops and excludes singleton signatures for additional nonconstant entries.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-opposite-partition)): Let $\gamma:\mathbb T\to\overline{\mathbb H}^{\,\infty}$ be continuous, and let $L$ be a coordinate line whose level is not a local extremal value of the corresponding coordinate of $\gamma$ at finite locations. For every bounded segment $K\subset L$, there is a finite parameter partition such that every piece meeting $K$ has endpoints on opposite strict sides of $L$ and lies in a bounded window. The other pieces stay off $K$ under sufficiently small uniform perturbations. The same assertion holds while retaining a prescribed bounded subpath with opposite-side endpoints as one unsplit piece, provided that subpath is considered separately when choosing the small-oscillation partition.
  - Explanation: Partitions relevant path portions into bounded pieces with opposite-side endpoints at generic test levels.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-identification)): There is no nonconstant entry with empty signature. Each remaining limiting path has exactly the trace of its corresponding loop of $\mathcal C$ and traverses it with absolute degree one.
  - Explanation: Excludes invisible nonconstant paths and identifies each remaining trace and its absolute degree.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-jordan-line)): Let $C$ be a Jordan curve, let $p\in C$, and let $L$ be a vertical or horizontal line through $p$. Suppose every open Jordan subarc around $p$ contains points on both strict sides of $L$. Then every neighborhood of $p$ along $L$ contains points in both complementary domains of $C$.
  - Explanation: Ensures both complementary Jordan domains meet a generic coordinate line arbitrarily near the selected point.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-backtracking)): Each path $\gamma_j$ is a weakly monotone once-around traversal of its canonical Jordan curve. In particular, it has zero curve distance from a simple parametrization of that curve.
  - Explanation: Excludes reverse passage and identifies limiting paths with simple curves in the loop metric.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interior-restriction)): Let $\mu_\delta$, $0<\delta\le1$, and $\mu$ be probability laws on $X$ supported on $S$. Suppose that $\mu_\delta\Rightarrow\mu$ in $X$ as $\delta\downarrow0$, that the family $(\mu_\delta)_{0<\delta\le1}$ is uniformly tight in $X$, and that $(\mu_\delta)_{a\le\delta\le1}$ is uniformly tight in $S$ for every $a>0$. Then $\mu_\delta\Rightarrow\mu$ in $S$, and the entire family is uniformly tight in $S$.
  - Explanation: Transfers ambient weak convergence and tightness to the interior collection subspace under the stated positive-mesh tightness hypotheses.

        "exact_quote": "Start with an ordinary two-replica cap vector. In each uncolored cap diagram specify a collection of eligible arches, and retain only diagrams having at most $M$ eligible arches.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rectangle-short",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R_\\delta$ be corner-rooted odd lattice rectangles converging to a fixed nondegenerate rectangle, with two independent uniform dimer covers. Fix an interior longitudinal coordinate $c$, two positive numbers $\\rho_-,\\rho_+$, and a bounded segment $J$ of the transverse line $x=c$. The two regions $x\\le c-\\rho_-$ and $x\\ge c+\\rho_+$ are called the deep regions; if either is disjoint from the rectangle, the events below are empty. The segment $J$ may extend beyond the transverse sides of the rectangle.*\n\n*For each sufficiently small $h>0$, partition $J$ into at most $C_J/h$ intervals of length at most $h$, with endpoints off the graph. Use a seam converging to $x=c$ and the corresponding intervals when necessary. A tested arc is a cycle subarc, in its cycle direction, with one endpoint in each deep region. Let $B_{\\delta,h}$ be the event that some partition interval $I$ satisfies at least one of the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *a tested arc $\\alpha$ on a cycle $g$ has $\\iota_I(\\alpha)\\ne0$ and $\\iota_I(\\alpha)\\ne\\iota_I(g)$;*\n\n2.  *tested arcs on two distinct cycles both have nonzero intersection number with $I$.*\n\n*The arcs may otherwise travel anywhere in the rectangle. Then $$\\lim_{h\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\n       \\mathbb P(B_{\\delta,h})=0.\n \\label{eq:rectangle-short-limit}$$ The conclusion is unchanged by truncating intervals at a transverse side, or by placing their endpoints outside the rectangle.",
        "source_id": "PM-226.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rectangle-short",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R_\\delta$ be corner-rooted odd lattice rectangles converging to a fixed nondegenerate rectangle, with two independent uniform dimer covers. Fix an interior longitudinal coordinate $c$, two positive numbers $\\rho_-,\\rho_+$, and a bounded segment $J$ of the transverse line $x=c$. The two regions $x\\le c-\\rho_-$ and $x\\ge c+\\rho_+$ are called the deep regions; if either is disjoint from the rectangle, the events below are empty. The segment $J$ may extend beyond the transverse sides of the rectangle.*\n\n*For each sufficiently small $h>0$, partition $J$ into at most $C_J/h$ intervals of length at most $h$, with endpoints off the graph. Use a seam converging to $x=c$ and the corresponding intervals when necessary. A tested arc is a cycle subarc, in its cycle direction, with one endpoint in each deep region. Let $B_{\\delta,h}$ be the event that some partition interval $I$ satisfies at least one of the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *a tested arc $\\alpha$ on a cycle $g$ has $\\iota_I(\\alpha)\\ne0$ and $\\iota_I(\\alpha)\\ne\\iota_I(g)$;*\n\n2.  *tested arcs on two distinct cycles both have nonzero intersection number with $I$.*\n\n*The arcs may otherwise travel anywhere in the rectangle. Then $$\\lim_{h\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\n       \\mathbb P(B_{\\delta,h})=0.\n \\label{eq:rectangle-short-limit}$$ The conclusion is unchanged by truncating intervals at a transverse side, or by placing their endpoints outside the rectangle.",
          "source_id": "PM-226.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sandwiched-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T_-$ and $\\mathcal T_+$ be the two-replica transfers from $a$ to $c$ and from $c$ to $b$, with column lengths $t_-$ and $t_+$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-color-contractions",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-walk-attachment",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le C_0<\\infty$. There are constants $C_1>C_0$ and $p>0$ with the following property. Let a square-lattice walk have mesh $a_0$, start at $x$ above a horizontal killing boundary, and let $a\\ge a_0$. Suppose an already explored nearest-neighbor path starts within distance $C_0a$ of $x$ and runs to that boundary. The probability that the walk hits the path or the killing boundary before leaving $B(x,C_1a)$ is at least $p$. The constants are independent of the path, the mesh, and the distance of $x$ from the boundary.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $u$ be either the horizontal or vertical coordinate, let $L=\\{u=c\\}$ be a test line, and let $J\\subset L$ be a bounded segment. Test lines are chosen off lattice vertices; the same estimate holds for seam perturbations of order $\\delta$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tightness",
        "exact_quote": "The laws of $F(\\mathcal L_\\delta)$ are tight in the collection space of closed-disk curves with the ensemble matching topology, with constant loops discarded. Moreover, the loops can be ordered by decreasing diameter and given measurable, orientation-preserving parametrizations whose joint laws are tight in the product of uniform path spaces. Every subsequential limit has finitely many loops above every positive diameter, and convergence retains all its nonconstant entries. At this stage limiting curves may be nonsimple, touch the boundary, or occur with multiplicity.",
        "source_id": "PM-226.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tightness",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **dimer covering** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A dimer covering is a perfect matching.
- **nested CLE** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Nested $\mathrm{CLE}_4$ means an ordinary nonnested $\mathrm{CLE}_4$, followed recursively by conditionally independent copies inside every loop.
- **compactification** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We fix, throughout, the compactification $$F:\mathbb H\longrightarrow\mathbb D,\qquad F(z)=\frac{z-i}{z+i}.$$
- **loop distance** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For continuous loops $\gamma,\widetilde\gamma:S^1\to\overline{\mathbb D}$, put $$d_{\rm loop}(\gamma,\widetilde\gamma) =\inf_{\phi}\sup_{t\in S^1} \|\gamma(t)-\widetilde\gamma(\phi(t))\|,$$ where $\phi$ ranges over circle homeomorphisms of either orientation.
- **matching of loop collections** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An $\varepsilon$-matching of two collections is a partial bijection covering every loop of diameter greater than $\varepsilon$ on either side, with matched loops at distance at most $\varepsilon$.
- **reduced lamination** ([source](#sec:inputs)): A lamination relative to a finite set of punctures records the homotopy classes of disjoint simple loops in the punctured domain, including their multiplicities. Delete every loop surrounding at most one puncture. The remaining record will be called the reduced lamination.
- **cap** ([source](#pm-h-9)): The part of the rectangle on either side of a seam is called a *cap*.
- **parameter-disjoint portions** ([source](#eq:seam-max-crossings)): Two path portions are called *parameter-disjoint* when their parameter interiors are disjoint; they may share endpoints.
- **tear** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A *tear* is the following formal modification of an arch. Instead of requiring opposite endpoint signs, prescribe both signs to be $+$, or both to be $-$, with weight one for that prescription.
- **signature** ([source](#pm-h-28)): We call $\{q\in Q:a_j(q)=1\}$ the *signature* of the entry $j$.
- **half-plane lattice** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $$\mathbb H=\{z\in\mathbb C:\operatorname{Im}z>0\},\qquad \mathbb H_\delta=\mathbb H\cap\delta\mathbb Z^2 .$$
- **unit disk** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\mathbb D=\{w\in\mathbb C:\|w\|<1\}$.
- **particle bit** ([source](#pm-h-10)): On a seam, define its particle bit $q_i$ to be $b_i$ if the site immediately to its left is black, and $1-b_i$ otherwise.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spectrum): Compatible singular bases and exterior powers -> Propagation penalties and summable states
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-cap-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cap-comparison): Explicit boundary wedge calculation -> Uniform cap normalization
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-rectangle-crossings) / [proof](#pm-h-14): Torn cap contractions and bounded slab recursion -> Traversal tightness
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-sandwiched-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sandwiched-insertion): Cauchy–Binet minors and propagation sums -> Quadratic short-interval insertion bound
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-chain-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-chain-positivity): Alternating chain sums with inward weight decay -> Nonnegative different-loop contribution
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-rectangle-short) / [proof](#pm-h-19): Positive weighted observable and interval union bound -> Short-segment exclusion
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-walk-attachment-2): Wilson exploration and planar duality -> Matching-state localization
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-opposite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-opposite): Preservation of local directed paths under coupling -> Half-plane opposite-sign exclusion
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tightness): Trip detection and measurable time changes -> Tightness of parametrized collections
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-visible-signatures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-visible-signatures): Fixed puncture records and conditional coupling -> Canonical visible signatures
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-identification): Slowly refining grids and indicator switches -> Trace and degree identification
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-backtracking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-backtracking): Opposite traversal indices on generic lines -> No backtracking
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-interior-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interior-restriction): Interior compact containment -> Weak convergence and uniform tightness in the interior collection subspace.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interior-restriction-2): Subsequence criterion and interior restriction -> The full mesh scaling limit with macroscopic curve matching.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-transfer**: The alternating exterior-power column transfers with the deleted-corner boundary states count single matchings; independent replicas give the square of that count.
- **I-curves**: The half-plane Temperleyan double-dimer ensemble converges to nested conformal loops in the stated collection topology, retaining every macroscopic curve.
- **I-local**: Localization couples matching states on a bounded closed window; it does not determine connections outside that window.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-finite**: Treat finite matching enumeration as a certificate of the continuum scaling limit. -> Exact finite counts do not decide the convergence of random curve collections.
- **A-tightness**: Infer simple interior curves from preliminary compactness alone. -> A tight subsequential collection can still contain boundary contact, repeated traces, or multiplicity.
- **A-local**: Use bounded-window agreement as a guarantee of global loop connections. -> Identical local directed paths may be connected differently outside the observation window.
- **A-order**: Exchange the interval and lattice refinement limits. -> The short-interval estimate does not supply the reversed order of limits.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite column transfer and its boundary contraction explicitly count matchings on an odd corner-rooted rectangle. This calculation does not certify the continuum curve limit.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with odd integer dimensions m,n on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= m,n <= 5; deleted corner (1,1); unit edge weights.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing exact single and two-replica matching counts.
# TIME: O(B^2*(p^3+1) + m*B^2) exact integer operations, B=binomial(n,p).
# SPACE: O(B^2+n^2) exact integers, excluding integer bit storage.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz determinants and integer accumulation; no rounding.
# FAILURE: invalid dimensions or malformed input exit nonzero.
import itertools
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpz, fmpz_mat

data = json.load(sys.stdin)
m, n = data['m'], data['n']
if type(m) is not int or type(n) is not int or not (1 <= m <= 5 and 1 <= n <= 5 and m % 2 == 1 and n % 2 == 1):
    raise ValueError('odd integer dimensions in admitted finite domain required')
p = (n - 1) // 2
states = list(itertools.combinations(range(1, n + 1), p))
A = [[int(i == j) + int(j % 2 == 1 and abs(i - j) == 1)
      for j in range(1, n + 1)] for i in range(1, n + 1)]
W = [[fmpz_mat([[A[i - 1][j - 1] for j in inp] for i in out]).det()
      if p else fmpz(1) for inp in states] for out in states]
alpha = tuple(range(3, n + 1, 2))
beta = tuple(range(2, n + 1, 2))
v = [fmpz(int(q == alpha)) for q in states]
for col in range(1, m + 1):
    v = [sum((W[i][j] if col % 2 else W[j][i]) * v[j]
             for j in range(len(states))) for i in range(len(states))]
Z = int(v[states.index(beta)])
print(json.dumps({'Z': Z, 'Z_dd': Z * Z}, sort_keys=True))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **law** (hypothesis): The scaling limit concerns independent matchings under the specified half-plane Temperleyan law. Other boundary laws and lattice models require separate justification. Blocked use: ['Using the convergence theorem for arbitrary matchings or lattices.']
- **curve-topology** (hypothesis): The convergence compares unrooted, unoriented curves after compactification and allows changes of parameter with pauses. It does not specify convergence of traversal times. Blocked use: ['Deriving physical timing or speed guarantees from unparametrized convergence.']
- **multiplicity** (edge_case): Collection comparison retains multiplicities and discards constant loops. A comparison that drops macroscopic entries changes the conclusion. Blocked use: ['Replacing ensemble matching by trace-set comparison without multiplicity.']
- **puncture-information** (counterexample_scope): Enclosed puncture subsets are a measurable reduction of lamination data and need not determine every homotopy class. Blocked use: ['Reconstructing complete homotopy information solely from enclosed subsets.']
- **limit-order** (approximation): The puncture configuration is fixed before selecting a sufficiently fine lattice mesh. The proof does not invoke puncture convergence with colliding or mesh-dependent punctures. Blocked use: ['Using simultaneous puncture and lattice refinement without the fixed-test selection.']
- **finite-rectangle** (hypothesis): Rectangle traversal tightness requires odd corner-rooted rectangles with a nondegenerate limiting shape, interior cuts and positive limiting separation. Blocked use: ['Applying the rectangle estimate to degenerating shapes or vanishing slab separation.']
- **cap-buffer** (hypothesis): Cap normalization requires the stated aspect-ratio and seam-buffer conditions. It does not give the same uniform comparison for arbitrary boundary-adjacent seams. Blocked use: ['Removing the longitudinal buffers from cap normalization.']
- **local-agreement** (hypothesis): Localization couples matching states on a bounded window; it does not determine the loop connections outside that window. Blocked use: ['Treating local matching agreement as agreement of complete loop identities.']
- **horizontal-height** (edge_case): The half-plane local traversal estimate assumes positive heights for horizontal detecting lines. Blocked use: ['Using the stated local traversal estimate at the killing boundary without an additional argument.']
- **signed-cancellation** (hypothesis): The insertion has signed entries. Its probability interpretation requires the planar positivity argument and the chosen nested cap weights. Blocked use: ['Treating an arbitrary signed contraction as a nonnegative event detector.']
- **preliminary-limit** (edge_case): Preliminary tightness permits nonsimple limiting curves, boundary contact and multiplicity. Trace identification and exclusion of backtracking are additional proof steps. Blocked use: ['Claiming simple interior limiting loops from preliminary compactness alone.']
- **degree-insufficient** (counterexample_scope): Trace equality and absolute degree one do not alone identify the class in the curve metric; reverse passage must also be excluded. Blocked use: ['Equating winding or trace agreement with unparametrized curve convergence.']
- **finite-check-scope** (formal_scope): Finite transfer and matching calculations can check algebraic instances. They do not certify the full mesh-limit theorem or provide a deterministic resource bound for the random ensemble. Blocked use: ['Claiming continuum convergence from finite enumeration.', 'Interpreting stochastic tightness as a deterministic runtime bound.']
- **constructive-rate** (unknown): ABSENT: an explicit mesh threshold and computational convergence rate for the complete loop ensemble. Blocked use: ['Selecting a certified finite mesh from a requested curve-error tolerance using this theorem alone.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the complete double-dimer loop ensemble for the Temperleyan square lattice in the upper half-plane converges to nested CLE<sub>4</sub>. The convergence holds along the full mesh limit and matches every macroscopic loop as an unparametrized curve. This resolves the half-plane Temperleyan form of the double-dimer CLE<sub>4</sub> scaling-limit conjecture.
